# 3D building data for Western and Southern Europe (UK, Ireland, Benelux, France, Switzerland, Austria, Iberia, Italy, the western Balkans, Greece, Cyprus)

Run date 2026-11-13. Read-only research (task #1325) for Scenic Roads. Method: a first pass of four sub-agents (Sonnet, medium) by area, then a second, gap-closing pass of four (Italy; UK, Ireland, Belgian and Dutch cities; Iberian city models, small states, Greece and the Balkans; Austrian Länder, Swiss cantons and the OSM height imports). Each fetched pages and API answers with User-Agent `scenic-roads/0.1 (personal offline map)`. I merged the rows by hand-run scripts into one table per region (the Americas report's 12 columns), the second pass's new rows appended to their region's table and its corrected rows replacing the first-pass rows in place, renumbered once, checked that every table row has its header's cell count (0 mismatches), read every merged main-table row, and wrote this headline from the finished tables. Germany, the Nordics and the east are task #1326. Global datasets (Microsoft, Google, GHSL, GBA, 3D-GloBFP, WSF3D, Esri, Cesium, Google 3D Tiles) are as in the Americas and US reports; only EUBUCCO and INSPIRE BU are added (section 3). Sections 4-16 are the region tables; 17 notes; 18 nothing better found; 19 the Overture sample and the OSM height imports; 20 overlaps; 21 Unverified; 22 accidental; 23 URLs.

## 1. Headline

- **Overture is nearly height-less in most of this region, so measured sources add a lot.** Height share in Overture boxes (section 19): Luxembourg 0.3 %, Lyon 0.5 %, Ljubljana 1.2 %, Zagreb 1.7 %, Paris 2.9 %, Brussels 3.3 %, Dublin 9.5 %, London 11.3 %, Manchester 22 %; high only in Amsterdam (77.4 %) and a rural Cotswolds box (62 %, Microsoft ML). Heights are Microsoft ML nearly everywhere, OSM where an import ran (Amsterdam: 3D BAG, done June 2026, 136,185 buildings, section 19.3; older ones in Rostock, Nice, Montpellier; Paris 2015 is storeys); in four Swiss boxes (Lausanne 54 %, Lugano 42 %, Sion 62 %, Chur 36 %) OSM heights are dense with no import page to explain them (unverified). Measured sources run 1.0-2.2 times Overture's heights (section 20), and their definitions differ (gutter, ridge, DSM minus terrain): choose one before mixing.
- **Open, national, measured heights beyond Overture:** France IGN BD TOPO (51.5 M buildings, `hauteur` = gutter height, filled on 93-99.6 % of sampled boxes, storeys on 45-74 %; Licence Ouverte 2.0; adds a height to 68-87 % of Overture's height-less buildings); Brussels UrbIS Constructions 3D (253,473 buildings, LoD2-equivalent, CC0; adds a height to 96 % of a box); Luxembourg ACT 2023 LoD2.2 (238,189 footprints, CC0; adds 93 %); Switzerland swissBUILDINGS3D 3.0 Beta (national LoD2 with roofs, free with source named, covers Liechtenstein); Austria BEV DLM Bauwerke (4.56 M LoD1 polygons, 99.98 % with a height, CC BY 4.0, but 9.9 % at a 4.5 m default), with Tyrol (494,079 roof polygons, 95.7 % with a height, CC BY 4.0; adds a height to 87 % of Innsbruck's height-less buildings), Vienna's Baukörpermodell (678,965, CC BY 4.0), Upper Austria, Styria, Graz (65,617, 86.7 % with a height; licence not found). The Netherlands' 3DBAG (10.78 M, CC BY 4.0) is already in Overture where the Amsterdam import ran (84 % of matched buildings equal to 0.12 m median) but not elsewhere: Rotterdam 3D (Public Domain Mark; 29,758 buildings in the central box, all with measuredHeight, storeys 85 %) adds a height to 88 % of its box (Overture heights 4.8 %).
- **Italy, new this pass:** Milan's DBT 2020 (237,743 volume units in 65,675 buildings, 99.9 % with a height, CC BY; adds a height to 26.6 % of Overture's buildings in its box, ratio 1.11) and 2012; Lombardy's Sondrio (199,491 units, 96.7 % of ground units with a height) and Lecco (175,889 units; ratio 1.44; adds 37 %) zips; Sardinia's urban DBGT (394 per-comune zips with eave and foot levels; Cagliari 54,411 units, level semantics inferred); Vicenza (27,613 buildings with 89.7 % heights, no geometry reachable); Tuscany's 40 municipal sets give only an eave elevation above sea level (a ground model is needed). Alto Adige and Basilicata are footprints only. Piedmont, Veneto ARPAV, Friuli-Venezia Giulia, Trentino, Turin, Marche, Campania and Calabria hosts time out from here (geoblocking not tested), Emilia-Romagna's WFS and Lazio's listing are 401, Puglia needs a login: those stay Unverified.
- **Storeys instead of heights:** Spain's Catastro INSPIRE (all but the Basque Country and Navarre): floors on 99.3 % of Madrid's 811,691 parts and 86.5 % of Barcelona's; licence text read: personal use fine, public use only of transformed products, no raw redistribution (a cache question more than an ODbL one). Barcelona's Overture floors are not an independent check: they are OSM `building:levels` set by the Spanish Cadastre Buildings Import (57 of 60 sampled ways cite it; section 22.3), so the Catastro adds little there (19 %) and much in Madrid (70.6 % of Overture buildings; only 7 of 60 sampled Madrid ways are import-tagged). Also: Switzerland's BFS building register (GWR, 3,374,916 points, floors on 65.2 % of existing ones, free with source named, 950 MB anonymous zip), Basel-Stadt's GWR export (32,262, floors on all), Slovenia KN STAVBE (1,173,180, storeys; 99.3 % in Ljubljana's box), Bizkaia (140,217, storeys on 139,609), Álava (81,467; heights are exactly 3 m per storey, derived), Gipuzkoa (125,206; estimated heights on 65 %), Navarra (511,295 polygons; storeys on 65 %), Cyprus DLS BU3 via a CC BY University of Cyprus GeoPackage (563,446, floors on 99.8 %), London's Building Stock Model v2 (floor counts, 90 % of Tower Hamlets TOIDs).
- **LoD2 and better, cities and regions:** Zagreb ZG3D 2022 (LoD2.2, 357,683 features, "Otvorena dozvola", text not read; adds a height to 98 % of its box); Madrid's city model (LoD2, CC BY 4.0, 2.2 GB SLPK not opened); Antwerp (1,118,070 roof-part polygons with Z, licence text unread; adds 95 %); Ghent (LoD2 DWG tiles, free reuse), Namur (LoD2.2 CityGML, CC BY, host timed out); Lyon (545,451 roofs; textured CityGML for 67 communes), Rennes (148,897 buildings, ODbL), Strasbourg (LoD2 OBJ), Lille (3D Tiles), Bordeaux (LoD2, 2012); Zurich city (current LoD2: 218,502 surfaces, 50,680 buildings), Geneva (LoD2, 81,053), Canton Bern heights (423,852; 89.9 %), Lucerne (123,365 block-model buildings; 4.87 M 3D objects); Catalonia's RTT 1:5000 (1,282,466 polygons all with `alcada`, CC BY 4.0, but outliers to 2,578 m and a 5.36 GB 2D download not taken); Glasgow (LoD1/LoD2, CC BY 4.0, 8,231 buildings verified); Salzburg city 3D model (download not found).
- **Not open or not usable:** the UK has no open per-building height product apart from London's Building Stock Model v1 (OGL; UPRN points keyed to OS TOIDs, height on 79 % of Tower Hamlets TOIDs; adds a height to 50 % of that box). OS Building Height Attribute (46,048,490) and OS NGD are under the PSGA Member Licence v3.0, read this pass: non-transferable, eligible public bodies only; OpenMap Local is OGL but footprint-only; the open route is Environment Agency 1 m lidar (OGL). Ireland's PRIME2 buildings (3,785,414) have no height; GeoDirectory heights (20 of 30 sample rows) are commercial; Dublin has only the CC BY-NC-ND Docklands model. Jersey's model is a sold product. Wallonia's LoD1 (SPW terms forbid redistribution; LoD2.2 unpublished); Flanders' 3D GRB LoD1 needs a login; Vaud's building heights are own use only. Croatia's DGU buildings (3,274,123) are footprints only; Portugal's Mapa de Construções (4,166,655) too; Málaga (64,304, CC BY-SA, no height). IGN France has no national LoD2 (LiDAR HD is a point cloud); BDNB copies BD TOPO heights and RNB has none; Kadaster's 3D Basisvoorziening is the same reconstruction as 3DBAG (yearly zips of 1.8-2.5 GB).
- **ODbL mixing:** CC0 (Brussels, Luxembourg, Alto Adige footprints), Public Domain Mark, Licence Ouverte 2.0, OGL and CC BY 4.0 are attribution-only; the OSM wiki rates CC BY 4.0 "likely incompatible" for contributing into OSM itself (a community page, not an OSMF ruling; the 3D BAG import needed a signed waiver), so keeping such data as a separate layer in a derived set is the cleaner reading. CC BY-SA, NC and ND sets (Málaga, Lecce, Dublin Docklands, Abruzzo) are out. EUBUCCO (ODbL, except Prague and Abruzzo) repackages old government sets (Flanders 2015, Wallonia 2014, Brussels 2012, Luxembourg 2013), so the originals are fresher.
- **Unverified and why:** the Italian hosts above; Greece (Hellenic Cadastre and geodata.gov.gr do not connect; ELSTAT publishes tables and a 1 km grid, not building points; Maroussi's `floors` is 0 on all 9,499); Montenegro, Bosnia and Herzegovina, North Macedonia (no downloadable building data); Albania and Kosovo (3D viewers or priced products only); Zaragoza (3D host dead, licence unread), Barcelona's 3D SLPK (downloads reset), Madrid's 3D heights (unopened), Malta (WMS-only on a host that does not connect), Andorra (form), Carinthia, Ticino, Valais and other Swiss cantons (unreached), Manchester, Birmingham, Edinburgh, Cardiff and Cambridge portals (no answer), Gibraltar, Guernsey, Isle of Man; the full list with routes is section 21.

## 2. Evidence caveats

Overlap boxes are single neighbourhoods of 0.05 degree (0.1 rural), and point-in-footprint matching undercounts on large cadastral blocks. Several licence texts were not read and are marked so in the rows (Zagreb, DLS, Antwerp, Namur, Salzburg, Graz, the Lombardy and Milan zips, Welsh Government tiles). Overture's own `height` definition was not re-read. Some overlap tables use different column sets (per-Overture-building views for building-part sources: Vienna, Zurich, Catastro); each states its method. Access notes: one agent's DNS failed and it resolved names over 1.1.1.1; the Catastro host omits its root certificate, so the root (fetched from the AIA address) was added to a trust file for that session, no verification turned off; Italian hosts that time out were not routed round. The second pass's Italian "Lombardy other provinces" and Emilia-Romagna rows are statements of absence, not data.

### 3.2 INSPIRE Buildings theme (BU)

- The INSPIRE data specification has a 2D and a 3D profile, and base and extended variants: "3D profile has same semantic content as the Buildings2D profile and allows in addition, the geometric representation of buildings in any of the four levels of detail of City GML"; the base model carries a `HeightAboveGround` data type with `Elevation`; the extended profiles add building units, installations and other constructions (read in the guideline's contents and scope text). So the 2D profile can carry a height value and the 3D profile adds LoD1-LoD4 geometry. Source: https://inspire-mif.github.io/technical-guidelines/data/bu/dataspecification_bu.html
- Austria: the BEV DLM "Bauwerke" record is tagged with the INSPIRE theme "Gebäude" and carries heights (LoD-1, 2.5D); its metadata includes a Conformity entry with the value `false`. Upper Austria's `GEBAEUDE_LOD1` is "served through INSPIRE" (GML 3.2, `InspireID` "bu...." on each polygon) and carries a relative height. Lille's "Maquette 3D" and "Hauteurs bâtiments" records carry the GEMET INSPIRE theme "Bâtiments". Sources: https://data.bev.gv.at/geonetwork/srv/ger/csw?service=CSW&version=2.0.2&request=GetRecordById&id=b7f4843a-83d1-419a-bc87-bc2aaa83976d&elementSetName=full&outputSchema=http://www.isotc211.org/2005/gmd ; https://e-gov.ooe.gv.at/at.gv.ooe.dorisdaten/DORIS_Basisdaten/GEBAEUDE_LOD1.zip ; https://www.data.gouv.fr/api/1/datasets/665fe74e5f3e53a1f79fc90e/
- France: no `BU.` layer in the Geoplateforme WFS capabilities (searched for "BU." and "inspire" in the layer names); BD TOPO is the practical national building source. Source: https://data.geopf.fr/wfs/ows?SERVICE=WFS&VERSION=2.0.0&REQUEST=GetCapabilities
- Switzerland and Liechtenstein are outside the EU and publish through swisstopo's STAC API, not INSPIRE (swisstopo's collection list has INSPIRE variants only for boundaries and names: `swissboundaries3d.inspire`, `swissnames3d.inspire`). Source: https://data.geo.admin.ch/api/stac/v1/collections?limit=100 (all pages)
- Not done: the EU INSPIRE geoportal's per-country BU dataset counts (its API was not reached with the queries tried).

## 9. France (with Monaco)

| Jurisdiction / product | Product, version | Publisher | Holds | Heights or storeys: how measured, what they mean | Completeness | Year | Format and access | Licence; cache?; ODbL-mixable? | Footprint/coverage-index URL | Vs Overture | Evidence URL(s) fetched |

| France / IGN BD TOPO, class `batiment` | BD TOPO 3.5 (content description dated July 2026; data.gouv record updated 2026-11-12, quarterly editions plus a weekly "Express" edition since May 2025) | IGN (Institut national de l'information géographique et forestière) | Footprints with a height attribute and storeys (3D multipolygon with Z, plus `hauteur`, `nombre_d_etages`, `nombre_de_logements`, wall/roof materials, `altitude_minimale_sol`, `altitude_maximale_toit`, `identifiants_rnb`, `construction_legere`) | `hauteur` = "Hauteur du bâtiment mesurée entre le sol et le point haut de la gouttière" (the highest point of the gutter; wall top if the roof is flat; lowest ground point if the terrain slopes), metres, so it excludes the roof volume. `nombre_d_etages` = "Nombre total d'étages du bâtiment, rez-de-chaussée compris", computed from the tax files (MAJIC): maximum(dnbniv, dniv+1); left empty for annexes, lightweight constructions and buildings that could not be matched. Altimetric precision follows the acquisition method: Lidar HD 0.5 m, Corrélation 1 m, Photogrammétrie 1.5 m, "Interpolation bâti BDTopo" 2.5 m, "Pas de Z" = Z unusable | 51,484,368 features in the WFS (hits query, 2026-11-13). Real sample, 4 boxes from the WFS: Paris 0.05° box 34,760 buildings, `hauteur` > 0 on 32,238 (92.7 %), `nombre_d_etages` on 25,563 (73.5 %); Lyon box 23,130 / 22,262 (96.2 %) / 14,696 (63.5 %); rural Cantal 0.1° box 1,989 / 1,929 (97.0 %) / 898 (45.1 %); Monaco-and-Menton box 4,063 / 4,046 (99.6 %) / 1,659 (40.8 %). Z method in Paris box: "Interpolation bâti BDTopo" 30,730 (88 %, 2.5 m class), "Pas de Z" 2,492 (7 %), Photogrammétrie 930; Lyon box: Corrélation 13,991 (60 %), Interpolation 7,244 (31 %), Pas de Z 865; Cantal: Lidar HD 30 buildings (origin "Lidar HD"). `construction_legere` true on 329 (Paris), 1,857 (Lyon), 815 (Monaco box). OSM wiki (TomTom page): Paris dep. 75 = 118,496 records, height and floors populated on 93.5 % | Object dates: Paris box mostly modified 2019 (73 %), then 2022, 2023; blank on 4,142; Cantal mostly 2022 | GeoPackage / SQL / Shapefile per département and per region (data.gouv resources), WFS `BDTOPO_V3:batiment` at data.geopf.fr (GeoJSON, paged 3,000 per page: used here), vector tiles | Licence Ouverte 2.0 (text read: reuse, copy, adapt and commercial use allowed with attribution; the text says it is designed to be compatible with CC-BY, OGL, ODC-BY); cache yes; mixable yes: the OSM wiki TomTom import page states "ODbL-compatible per OSMF" for BD TOPO under Licence Ouverte 2.0 (page read; a community page, not an OSMF ruling) | https://data.geopf.fr/wfs/ows?service=WFS&version=2.0.0&request=GetFeature&typeNames=BDTOPO_V3:batiment&resultType=hits ; https://geoservices.ign.fr/telechargement?bdd%5B0%5D=233&bdd%5B1%5D=233 | Better for height wherever it has it. Overture France footprints are OpenStreetMap (Paris box 30,066 of 30,277; Lyon 22,676 of 22,800; Cantal 1,928 of 2,084), so BD TOPO is a different source. Overlap (section 6): BD TOPO adds a height to 87.0 % of the Overture buildings that have none in the Paris box (84.4 % of all Overture buildings there), 85.9 % in Lyon (85.7 % of all), 68.6 % in rural Cantal; matched heights are gutter heights, so they sit below roof-inclusive heights (Paris ratio to Overture's heights 1.15, Lyon 1.02 from only 19 pairs, Cantal 1.40) | https://data.geopf.fr/wfs/ows?service=WFS&version=2.0.0&request=GetFeature&typeNames=BDTOPO_V3:batiment&resultType=hits ; https://data.geopf.fr/wfs/ows?service=WFS&version=2.0.0&request=GetFeature&typeNames=BDTOPO_V3:batiment&outputFormat=application/json&srsName=EPSG:4326&bbox=48.84,2.33,48.89,2.38,urn:ogc:def:crs:EPSG::4326&count=3000 (and the Lyon, Cantal, Monaco boxes) ; https://data.geopf.fr/annexes/ressources/documentation/DC_BDTOPO_3-5.pdf ; https://www.data.gouv.fr/api/1/datasets/61489083dc4223219e50cc35/ ; https://www.etalab.gouv.fr/wp-content/uploads/2017/04/ETALAB-Licence-Ouverte-v2.0.pdf ; https://wiki.openstreetmap.org/w/index.php?title=Organised_Editing/Activities/TomTom/Global_-_Building_Height_Imports/France_-_IGN_BD_TOPO_Building_Heights_Import&action=raw |

| Austria / BEV Digitales Landschaftsmodell (DLM) "Bauwerke" (INSPIRE theme Buildings) | `DLM_8000_BAUWERK`, Stichtag 23.03.2026 (metadata record stamped 2026-06-12; earlier cut-offs 25.01.2023, 12.09.2023, 18.11.2024, 25.03.2025, 29.09.2025 are also in the catalogue) | BEV (Bundesamt für Eich- und Vermessungswesen) | Footprints with several height attributes ("Blockmodell in 2.5D, LoD-1"): ground height min/median/max (`HOEHE_BODEN_MIN`, `_MEDIAN`, `_MAX`), object height median and maximum (`HOEHE_OBJEKT_MEDIAN`, `HOEHE_OBJEKT_MAX`), capture method per height, link to the address register (AGWR object number) | `HOEHE_OBJEKT_MEDIAN` = "Median aller innerhalb des Polygons ermittelten Bauwerkshöhen", relative, from the aerial-image DSM (DOM) minus the lowest ground height, decimetre accuracy; in Vienna taken from the city's FMZK (lowest terrain height "T_KOTE" converted from Wiener Null by +156.68 m), in Graz from the city's surveying office. Sources: orthophotos, land-cover classification, airborne laser scanning, DSM image matching, DKM (cadastre), AGWR | 4,558,452 polygons in `BWK_8100_BAUWERK_F` (sqlite count of the downloaded file); `HOEHE_OBJEKT_MEDIAN` > 0 on 4,557,759 (99.98 %), empty on 690; `HOEHE_OBJEKT_TRAUFE` (eaves) > 0 on only 269,445 (5.9 %). Method of the median height: DOM image-matching surface model 3,758,000 (82.4 %), "Default" 452,162 (9.9 %: the description says "Default Fixwert (4,5 m)", a fixed value, not a measurement), city data of Wien 263,262, Graz 46,891, Salzburg 37,447 (LoD2 CityGML). Footprint capture: DKM 3,594,975, DOP 488,359, Wien 264,085, Graz 58,970, Linz 46,207, Salzburg 37,380. Overflight year (`BEFLIEGUNGSJAHR`): 2016: 825,736; 2018: 725,399; 2017: 563,893; 2015: 444,426; 2020: 443,544; 2024: 263,388; empty 663,419. AGWR address link: 2,098,846 "AGWR Aktiv" plus smaller classes, 2,374,156 without unique match. A 1-in-20 row sample: median of `HOEHE_OBJEKT_MEDIAN` 5.6 m. | Spain (all but Basque Country and Navarre) / Catastro INSPIRE Buildings | BU ATOM, 56 territorial-office feeds, municipality zips, feed updated 2026-08-21 | Dirección General del Catastro | Footprints with a height attribute (storeys): one BuildingPart per volume with numberOfFloorsAboveGround and numberOfFloorsBelowGround, plus Building with construction date and use | Storeys above and below ground per part, from the cadastral record (a count, not metres); a Building (cadastral reference) has several parts. Counted: Madrid (28900) 811,691 parts in 124,397 buildings, 805,635 parts (99.3 %) with floors above ground >0, median 4, max 59, 383,855 with basement floors >0; Barcelona (08900) 319,559 parts in 69,911 buildings, 276,506 (86.5 %) floors >0 (43,053 parts have 0); Soria city (42900) 19,840 parts in 3,849 buildings, 19,764 (99.6 %) floors >0; Ajalvir (28002) 4,629 parts in 1,296 buildings, 4,533 (97.9 %) | All Spain except País Vasco and Navarra (feeds for 56 offices); urban and rural; storeys on 86-99.6 % of parts in the five municipalities sampled | Cadastral reference 2012+, files regenerated twice a year (2026-08-21) | ATOM feeds per province; per-municipality zip of GML (building, buildingpart, otherconstruction; EPSG:25830, 25831 for Catalonia/Balearics); Madrid's zip 117 MB, GML 2.6 GB unzipped; WFS and WMS too | Licencia de acceso y uso INSPIRE (Jul 2016), text read: free download and use for oneself or for new value-added products if the information is transformed; original information may not be redistributed untransformed; derived products must not be named "cartografía catastral"; ATOM feed rights line: free if D.G. del Catastro is cited as author and owner. Personal offline cache: yes ("uso propio"). ODbL-mixable: floors extracted into a derived set are a transformation, which the licence allows to be used publicly including commercially; cite the Catastro; redistribution of the raw GML is not allowed | https://www.catastro.hacienda.gob.es/INSPIRE/buildings/ES.SDGC.BU.atom.xml (per-office feeds linked inside; e.g. .../buildings/28/ES.SDGC.bu.atom_28.xml) | Adds storeys where Overture has neither height nor floors: Madrid box 15,541 of 22,018 Overture buildings (70.6 %), Barcelona box 5,211 of 26,914 (19.4 %), Soria box 1,703 of 4,429 (38.5 %); see section 5. Barcelona's Overture floors equal Catastro's on 89 % of buildings, Madrid's on 45 %. Origin of Overture's Barcelona floors settled: of 60 sampled OSM ways with Overture num_floors in the Barcelona box, 57 first got building:levels in changesets citing the Spanish Cadastre import, so that 89 % agreement is by origin; in the Madrid box the same sample gives 7 of 60 (the rest are StreetComplete surveys and hand edits), and Overture's IGN Espana records carry 801 of Madrid's 4,315 num_floors (section 7). | Jurisdiction / product | Product, version | Publisher | Holds | Heights or storeys: how measured, what they mean | Completeness | Year | Format and access | Licence; cache?; ODbL-mixable? | Footprint/coverage-index URL | Vs Overture | Evidence URL(s) fetched |

| Slovenia / Kataster nepremičnin, layer STAVBE (buildings) | live WFS 2.0, layer SI.GURS.KN:STAVBE; metadata revised 2026-08-17 (`DATUM_SYS` of a sampled row 2024-08-29) | Geodetska uprava RS (GURS) | footprints with a height attribute; storeys | `STEVILO_ETAZ` = number of storeys (register field). `VISINA_H1` lowest, `VISINA_H2` highest, `VISINA_H3` "characteristic" height, all absolute elevations above sea level (GURS study: "H1 - najnižja nadmorska višina stavbe, H2 - najvišja nadmorska višina stavbe, H3 - karakteristična nadmorska višina"); building height = H2 minus H1 (my derivation; H2 includes a chimney per the same study). Each row also carries `VISINSKA_NATANCNOST_STAVBE_ID` (height accuracy class, e.g. 1 = "do ±0,25 m, višine določene z meritvami"), `LETO_IZGRADNJE`, `STEVILO_STANOVANJ`, `BRUTO_TLORISNA_POVRSINA` | 1,173,180 buildings (WFS numberMatched of STAVBE; 1,173,178 in STAVBE_OBRIS outlines). Ljubljana box 14.48-14.53 x 46.03-46.08: 21,341 buildings, 21,200 (99.3 %) with storeys, 6,911 (32.4 %) with H1 and H2 giving a positive height, 21,339 with a build year. 21,258 have an outline polygon, 83 only a point. ## 17. Notes per jurisdiction

### 17.1 UK, Ireland, Benelux

United Kingdom. No open per-building height product exists for Great Britain. OS publishes it (BHA, 46,048,490 features; NGD with heights, storeys and confidence) but only under PSGA or paid licences; OpenMap - Local is open and has no height. The open route is lidar: the EA National LIDAR Programme (1 m DSM and DTM for all of England, OGL) and the Scottish lidar (OGL, partial); Overture's heights in the English boxes are Microsoft ML (92 % in London, 97.5 % in Manchester). Cityweft's December 2025 post shows a commercial reseller doing exactly that derivation for England. Glasgow City has a CC BY 4.0 LoD1/LoD2 model (Zenodo, v4). I found no open city 3D model for London, Bristol, Cambridge, Birmingham, Edinburgh, Cardiff or Belfast (nothing-better table).

Ireland. Tailte Eireann's building layer (3,785,414 polygons, CC BY 4.0) is footprints only. Heights and floors for every building exist only commercially (GeoDirectory GeoBuilding Intel). Overture's Dublin box: 9.5 % with height (Microsoft ML 98 % of those), 68 % with neither height nor floors.

Netherlands. 3DBAG is the best open building dataset in the region: LoD1.2/1.3/2.2 for about 10.8 million BAG buildings under CC BY 4.0, WFS with bbox queries, tiles, 3D Tiles, IFC. Overture already mirrors its heights in OSM in Amsterdam (84 % of matched buildings, 0.12 m median difference), so the incremental value is the missing 17.5 %, storeys (b3_bouwlagen, up to 5 floors, 74 % of Amsterdam buildings), and LoD2 roof shapes if wanted. PDOK's Kadaster 3D Basisvoorziening is a second, yearly route (also CC BY 4.0).

Belgium. Three regions, three situations. Brussels: UrbIS Constructions 3D, LoD2-equivalent, CC0, monthly, whole region, 253,473 buildings, the cleanest source in this report; Overture has a height on only 3.3 % of the Brussels box. Flanders: 3D GRB LoD1 from 2013-2016 lidar, open data licence, download behind a registration login (not bypassed). Wallonia: INSPIRE LoD1 2013-2014 under the SPW CGU (no redistribution); a LoD2.2 model from lidar 2021-2022 has been produced (slides) but not published. Footprints: the PICC (3,894,749 building polygons) is better than Overture's but under the same CGU.

Luxembourg. ACT's 2023 national 3D buildings (LoD2.2, CC0, 238,189 footprints, per-building height in the INSPIRE GML) are the most complete open Luxembourg source and adds a height to 93 % of buildings in the central box.

EUBUCCO. v0.2 on s3.eubucco.com (322 M buildings in 55 datasets): government heights only where governments publish them (NL, BE, LU in this area; none for IE or UK); the rest are OSM, Microsoft and ML estimates. It repackages the same old government 3D sets (Flanders 2015, Wallonia 2014, Brussels 2012, Luxembourg 2013) and is therefore less current than the originals above. Licence: ODbL v1.0 except Prague (CC-BY-SA) and Abruzzo (CC-BY-NC).

INSPIRE Buildings theme. The harmonised BU 3D download is real for Luxembourg (INSPIRE Building 3D GML with heightAboveGround) and Wallonia (bu-core3d LoD1 GML); for the others no INSPIRE route was reached (Unverified).

Overture's treatment. In every sampled box (23 boxes, section 7) footprints come from OpenStreetMap and Microsoft ML Buildings, plus IGN Espana in Spain; heights come from Microsoft ML (the `height` property entry) or the OSM record. No Dutch, Belgian, Luxembourg, Irish or British national dataset appears under its own name. A wider scan of dataset names (every 20th building in up to 5 row groups per part, 401 row groups overlapping lon -11..36, lat 34..62) found, as footprint supplier: OpenStreetMap 280,228 sampled, Microsoft ML Buildings 174,462 (36,842 with a height entry), Instituto Geografico Nacional (Espana) 18,570, Google Open Buildings 7,171 (license null), Esri Community Maps 5,277 (1,340 with a height entry; license "CC BY 4.0 with OpenStreetMap waivers"); "USGS Lidar" 67 height entries also appear in this wide box (its region was not located; likely outside Europe proper, e.g. Greenland or north Africa, inside the rectangle).
### 17.2 France, Switzerland, Austria

- **France.** BD TOPO is the only national, free, building-by-building height and storey source; its `hauteur` is a gutter height (documentation text read), which the OSM wiki's TomTom page says underestimates an OSM-style total height by more than 3 m for pitched Paris roofs. Roughly 88 % of Paris-box buildings have a Z interpolated from the old BD TOPO (2.5 m precision class), 60 % of the Lyon box a correlation-based Z (1 m class), and only the buildings processed from LiDAR HD (30 of 1,989 in the Cantal box) have the 0.5 m class. Storeys come from tax files, so are missing for annexes and unmatched buildings (45 % filled in rural Cantal, 74 % in Paris). LoD2 and textured meshes exist only for individual metropolises and regional pilots (Lyon, Strasbourg, Rennes, Bordeaux, Lille, Brest, Isère/Clermont/Riom); IGN publishes no national LoD2 (data.gouv search for IGN 3D buildings and the WFS layer list returned none; LiDAR HD is point cloud and rasters).
- **Monaco.** Inside BD TOPO (1,026 buildings in the Monte-Carlo sub-box); Monaco's own open-data host answered 503 / certificate failure and the geoportail page is parcels and planning rules only.
- **Switzerland.** swissBUILDINGS3D 3.0 Beta is national LoD2 with roofs under open terms (source mention); only the EGID areas (19 cantons and Zurich city) have a computed building height and CityGML. Cantons Bern and Lucerne publish LiDAR-derived building heights on cadastral footprints; Geneva has LoD2 plus a footprint layer with `HAUTEUR` and levels; Zurich city has annual LoD2 roof models. Overture's Microsoft-derived heights are low by a factor of 1.7 to 2.2 in the three Swiss boxes (Zurich, Bern, Geneva) against total heights.
- **Liechtenstein.** swissBUILDINGS3D 3.0 Beta and swissTLM3D both state Liechtenstein coverage; no separate national product looked for beyond that.
- **Austria.** BEV's DLM Bauwerke is a single national CC BY 4.0 GeoPackage (4,558,452 polygons, 2.65 GB) with a height on 99.98 % of them (LoD-1), but 9.9 % of the median heights are the fixed default 4.5 m and only 5.9 % carry an eaves height; heights come from an image-matching surface model, except in Vienna, Graz and Salzburg where the cities' own data are used; Vienna's FMZK Baukörpermodell is the metre-accurate city layer (eaves heights, parts); Upper Austria and Styria add Land-level height layers; Linz has LoD2.
- **Heights are not one quantity.** BD TOPO = gutter; Vienna Baukörper = eaves (Traufe); Zurich and Geneva 3D = ridge minus lowest ground; Bern and Lucerne = DSM minus terrain; Upper Austria and Styria = DSM minus terrain (relative). A mixing rule has to pick one definition.
### 17.3 Iberia and Italy

Spain. The Catastro is the national answer for storeys. Parts, not buildings, carry them, and Barcelona has 13.5 % parts with 0 floors above ground (patios and similar). Navarre and the Basque provinces publish their own cadastres; Navarra's are Roman-numeral labels, Gipuzkoa's are INSPIRE buildings with estimated heights and storeys. The Catastro licence allows personal use and public use of transformed products, not redistribution of the raw files, which matters for any build that ships data. Zaragoza's LoD2 3D Tiles and CityGML database are the only real LoD2 found, built from Catastro footprints and PNOA 2023 LiDAR, but its host was unreachable.

Portugal. DGT's 2024 LiDAR is open under CC-BY 4.0 (10 points/m²) and the DGT building-polygon product has no heights; heights would have to be derived from the point cloud or the MDS.

Italy. There is no national height layer. The useful finds are municipal and regional: Bologna (65,735 volumes with eave heights, open, CC BY 4.0) and Ferrara (32,058 buildings with DSM-derived heights). Regional DBT "unità volumetriche" exist (Emilia-Romagna, Piemonte, Lombardy-Sondrio, Tuscany municipalities) but each was blocked, gated by an order form, or unreachable in this run.
### 17.4 Slovenia to Cyprus

- **Slovenia.** The strongest source of the area: a national register of 1.17 M buildings under CC BY, storeys on nearly all and absolute elevations H1/H2/H3 on about a third of the sampled urban buildings. The WFS blocks attribute filters (403), so a country share of buildings with heights was not counted, only the Ljubljana box. Height meaning: H2 is the highest point including chimneys; H1 can be below ground for buildings with basements (study text), so H2 minus H1 can overstate. Heights "determined by measurement" (accuracy class 1) in the sampled rural row.
- **Croatia.** Zagreb's city model is the one open LoD2-class model in this area; it is a multipatch in the FeatureServer but the downloads flatten it to attributes (Z_Min/Z_Max/Z_Delta); a true roof mesh would need the FGDB. The national cadastral buildings (3.27 M) carry no height.
- **Cyprus.** One 563 k building set with storeys on 99.8 % but a derived copy (Zenodo) of the DLS service; licence of the primary not read.
- **Albania, Kosovo.** 3D products exist but only as viewers or priced products.
- **Serbia.** Not searched (out of scope).

## 19. Non-governmental sources and the Overture sample

Method: S3 listing https://overturemaps-us-west-2.s3.amazonaws.com/?list-type=2&prefix=release/2026-09-23.1/theme%3Dbuildings/type%3Dbuilding/ read with pyarrow's anonymous S3 filesystem (512 parts); the footer of every part was read for per-row-group bbox statistics (bbox.xmin/ymin/xmax/ymax min and max), 41 s, saved as overture-index.json (10.9 MB, one entry per part with row-group ids, rows and bbox ranges, plus the 23 boxes with the parts and row groups used). For each box only the row groups whose bbox statistics overlap the box were read, columns id, bbox, height, num_floors, sources. Buildings counted when the bbox min corner lies in the box (0.05 degree urban; 0.1 degree rural). "Height supplied by": the source entry whose property contains "height" if present, otherwise the footprint's dataset; footprint dataset = the sources entry with an empty property. Part numbers by region: UK/Ireland 112-126 (Dublin 115, Lisbon 112, Madrid 116, Soria 117, Manchester 119, London 125, Cotswolds 126), France/Italy/Switzerland/Austria/Balkans 193-208 (Barcelona 193, Rome 198, Molise 199, Lyon and Cantal 200, Zurich 203, Milan 204, Ljubljana 206, Vienna and Zagreb 207, Athens 208), Cyprus 219, Benelux 226 (Luxembourg), 227 (Brussels), 224 (Paris), 232 (Amsterdam). Bytes read not tracked (footers about 0.5 GB in all, each box a few MB). Dataset names present in all 23 boxes: OpenStreetMap, Microsoft ML Buildings (both license ODbL-1.0) and Instituto Geografico Nacional (Espana) (license null; Madrid, Barcelona, Soria only, footprints only, never a height). Evidence: the listing URL and the part files under that prefix.

| Region and box | File | Row groups read | Buildings | With height | With num_floors | No height and no floors | Height supplied by (share of buildings with a height); footprints by |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

Reading the table. (1) Microsoft ML is the height supplier in almost every box (London 92 %, Manchester 97.5 %, Brussels 97.8 %, Rome 95.7 %, Zurich 98.9 %, Molise 100 %); in Dutch Amsterdam the heights are in the OSM record (31,497 of 31,621, 77.4 % of buildings; section 8 shows they equal 3DBAG heights to a median 0.12 m). Where the OSM community maps heights (Vienna 1,720 of 2,754) OSM supplies them. (2) Heights are scarce in Lyon (0.5 %), Luxembourg (0.3 %), Ljubljana (1.2 %), Zagreb (1.7 %), Brussels (3.3 %), Athens (4.0 %), Lisbon (3.3 %), Paris (2.9 %), Nicosia (2.4 %): there a national height product adds the most. (3) num_floors is high where OSM has building:levels (Paris 65 %, Vienna 77 %, Barcelona 75 %, London 42.5 %) and mostly independent of height: only the share with neither is the real gap (London 10,525 = 50 %, Dublin 30,410 = 68 %, Brussels 50,774 = 94 %, Athens 43,192 = 92 %, Lyon 16,077 = 70 %). (4) Rural boxes have fewer buildings, and a higher height share where Microsoft covers them (Cotswolds 62 %, Molise 84 %). (5) IGN Espana contributes 1,022 footprints in Madrid, 321 in Barcelona, 1,566 in Soria, with no height. (6) Caveat: boxes are single neighbourhoods; a box straddling part boundaries is read through the row-group statistics of all parts, so counts should be complete, but a box whose row-group statistics are null would have been read in full (none occurred).

### 19.3 OSM building-height imports from government datasets (Europe)

Pages read: the TomTom project page and its four sub-project pages, the OSM wiki Import/Catalogue page (raw wikitext, 199 KB, every row mentioning building, heights, levels or a European country read), and the pages of the older imports named below, all through https://wiki.openstreetmap.org/w/index.php?action=raw&title=... Status is as the page states it; the sub-pages carry dates up to 24 June 2026 (France page marked "last synced with FR version 2026-06-23").

Switzerland and Austria also have no page in the wiki's Switzerland/Datasources list that names a height import. Overture's OSM heights are nevertheless dense in four Swiss boxes (Lausanne 53.8 %, Lugano 42.0 %, Sion 62.0 %, Chur 35.6 % of buildings; 99 % with decimals; the OSM edits date mostly 2023-2026, in blocks: Lugano 2,642 in 2025 and 1,759 in 2026, Chur 1,522 in 2023): batches of machine-derived heights of unknown source, not documented in the catalogue (unverified) | https://wiki.openstreetmap.org/w/index.php?action=raw&title=Import/Catalogue (every row mentioning building, height, levels or a European country read) ; https://wiki.openstreetmap.org/w/index.php?action=raw&title=Switzerland/Datasources ; wiki search for swissBUILDINGS3D height, Gebäudehöhe Import Schweiz, building heights import Lugano (no import pages) ; Overture boxes |

Answer to the question asked: in Europe, Overture's OSM heights are already a government dataset only in the Amsterdam municipality (3D BAG, done June 2026) and, from older imports, in Rostock (2009 heights), Nice and Montpellier (2017 DSM-minus-DTM maximum heights) and Paris (2015 storeys, not metres). Paris (BD TOPO), Berlin (Umweltatlas) and the rest of the Netherlands are still planned or at pilot size, so a nationwide height layer in those places is still a separate source. The Overture release 2026-09-23.1 reads OSM planet data of 2026-09-06, after the Amsterdam rollout.

## 20. Overlap with Overture (what each source would add)

### 20.1 Netherlands, Belgium, Luxembourg

Method as 13.3 of the Americas report (overlap_tool.overlap): source polygon -> ST_PointOnSurface -> smallest Overture footprint containing it (duckdb spatial, release 2026-09-23.1, same boxes as section 7). "Adds a height" = source has a height and the matched Overture building has none, or no Overture footprint matches. Pair statistics only where both have a height. Height for 3DBAG = (b3_h_max or b3_h_70p) minus b3_h_maaiveld; for UrbIS = highest ROOFSURFACE z minus ground z; for Luxembourg = INSPIRE heightAboveGround (highest point minus lowest ground point).

| Source and box | Source buildings | Matched to an Overture footprint | Matched with an Overture height (by supplier) | Source buildings that add a height Overture lacks | Height comparison where both | Bytes read (source) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 3DBAG 2025.09.03 (WFS lod12), Amsterdam 4.87..4.92 x 52.35..52.40 | 38,482 features (38,319 unique BAG ids; all with h_max; 28,517 with b3_bouwlagen) | 37,650 (97.8 %); 832 unmatched | 31,736 = 84.3 % of matched: OSM 31,690, Microsoft ML 46 | 6,746 = 17.5 % (h_max); storeys (b3_bouwlagen) where Overture has neither height nor floors: 1,737 | 31,736 pairs, h_max version: median abs diff 0.12 m, signed (3DBAG minus Overture) +0.05 m, median ratio 1.005, within 20 % 99.2 %. 70th-percentile version: median abs 0.89 m, signed -0.87 m, ratio 0.933, within 20 % 94.6 % | 8 WFS pages of 5,000 features, about 90 MB of GeoJSON (deleted) |
| UrbIS Constructions 3D (20261107 GPKG), Brussels 4.33..4.38 x 50.82..50.87 | 55,015 buildings (55,009 with a roof z) | 53,568 (97.4 %); 1,447 unmatched | 1,835 = 3.4 % of matched: Microsoft ML 1,802, OSM 33 | 53,042 = 96.4 % | 1,835 pairs: median abs diff 5.91 m, signed +5.86 m, median ratio 1.59, within 20 % 14.0 % (UrbIS height is highest roof point above lowest ground; Microsoft ML heights look low) | 488 MB zip (deleted) |
| ACT 3D buildings 2023 (INSPIRE GML + footprints GPKG), Luxembourg 6.10..6.15 x 49.58..49.63 | 17,082 footprints in the box, of which 15,940 (the Luxembourg commune) have the GML height | 15,995 (93.6 %); 1,087 unmatched | 31 = 0.2 % of matched (all OSM); num_floors on 2,011 matched | 15,909 = 93.1 % of source, 99.8 % of source heights | 31 pairs only: median abs 9.95 m, signed +9.95 m, ratio 1.55, within 20 % 29 % (too few to judge) | 531 MB GML + 63 MB zip (deleted) |

Reading it. Brussels and Luxembourg are almost pure gains: a national or regional LoD2 height on 93-96 % of their buildings, against Overture's 3 % and 0.3 %. Amsterdam shows the opposite case: Overture already carries 3DBAG-equivalent heights on 84 % of matched buildings (median difference 0.12 m with the roof-maximum definition), so only the 17.5 % of buildings without one are gains, and the storeys estimate (b3_bouwlagen, up to 5 floors) adds 1,737. Note the height definition matters: the roof maximum matches Overture's OSM heights; the 70th percentile is 0.9 m lower. In Brussels, Overture's Microsoft heights run about 40 % below UrbIS's highest roof point; an offset or definition choice is needed before mixing (as for NRCan in the Americas report). Caveats: boxes are single neighbourhoods; point matching on large building blocks may undercount matches; the Luxembourg box straddles communes (only the commune of Luxembourg's GML was fetched); an earlier attempt that took the centre of each building's 3D vertices gave 18 % matches and was discarded for the footprint polygons; UrbIS "height" uses the highest roof face, not an eaves height; the 3DBAG WFS holds building parts as separate features (163 duplicates by BAG id in the box).

Method: source building -> one representative point (London: mean of the building's UPRN points; Antwerp: point on the largest roof part; Rotterdam: point on the BAG polygon) -> the smallest Overture footprint containing it (duckdb spatial, Overture 2026-09-23.1 read from S3, 0.05-degree box). "Adds a height" = the source has a height and the matched Overture building has none, or nothing matches. Overture's supplier of a height is the `/properties/height` source entry, "other (OSM record)" where the entry is absent (the OSM record carries the value).

| Source and box | Source buildings | Matched to an Overture footprint | Matched with an Overture height (by supplier) | Source buildings that add a height or storeys Overture lacks | Height comparison where both | Bytes read (source) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| London Building Stock Model v1, Tower Hamlets -0.07..-0.02 x 51.50..51.55 (Overture box: 19,685 buildings, 4,830 with a height = 24.5 %, 9,978 with num_floors) | 21,803 TOIDs with a point in the box (17,293 with a height, 16,968 with a floor count) | 17,729 (81.3 %); 4,074 unmatched; matched footprints OSM 17,513, Microsoft ML 216 | 7,270 = 41.0 % of matched: Microsoft ML 7,057, other (OSM record) 213 | height: 10,882 = 49.9 % of source; storeys where Overture has neither height nor floors: 6,150 = 28.2 % | height, 6,411 pairs: median abs diff 1.18 m, signed (LBSM minus Overture) +0.26 m, median ratio 1.03, within 20 % 62.9 %; floors, 6,946 pairs: equal 62.5 %, within 1 storey 93.0 %, median ratio 1.00 | 51 MB CSV (deleted) |
| Antwerp LoD2 roof parts, central box 4.38..4.43 x 51.19..51.24 (Overture box: 37,190 buildings, 2,070 with a height = 5.6 %, 975 with num_floors) | 38,704 buildings (BUILDINGID) with the point in the box, from 262,937 roof parts (38,943 buildings touch the envelope); every one has roof Z | 36,808 (95.1 %); 1,896 unmatched; matched footprints OSM 36,785, Microsoft ML 23 | 1,949 = 5.3 % of matched: Microsoft ML 913, other (OSM record) 1,036 | 36,755 = 95.0 % of source | roof Z is metres TAW, so a ground height was read for a random 700 buildings from the city's raster layer 16 (all 700 found): height above ground median 13.36 m, p90 18.9 m; 45 of them have an Overture height: median abs diff 0.63 m, signed -0.11 m, ratio 0.99, within 20 % 71 % (too few pairs to judge) | 262,937 roof polygons as GeoJSON pages plus 700 identify calls |
| Rotterdam 3D LoD2, box 4.46..4.51 x 51.90..51.95 (Overture box: 28,309 buildings, 1,823 with a height = 6.4 %, 1,187 with num_floors) | 27,682 BAG panden (PDOK WFS polygons) with the point in the box, 25,818 of which carry Rotterdam 3D attributes by BAG id (measuredHeight, AANTAL_BOUWLAGEN); the 358 box tiles hold 29,758 ids | 27,181 (98.2 %); 501 unmatched; matched footprints OSM 27,178, Microsoft ML 3 | 1,304 = 4.8 % of matched: Microsoft ML 1,013, other (OSM record) 291 | height: 24,468 = 88.4 % of the 27,682; storeys where Overture has neither: 23,488 = 84.8 % | height, 1,224 pairs: median abs diff 2.28 m, signed (Rotterdam minus Overture) +1.10 m, ratio 1.14, within 20 % 40.0 %; floors, 1,196 pairs: equal 41.8 %, within 1 storey 72.9 %, ratio 1.00 | 358 b3dm tiles and 30 WFS pages of BAG polygons |

Reading it. Rotterdam and Antwerp are near pure gains: a LoD2 height on 88-95 % of the buildings in a central box against 5-6 % in Overture, and Rotterdam also gives storeys (up to 102) where Overture has them on 4 %. Rotterdam's heights run 14 % above Overture's (Microsoft and OSM) where both exist; the Antwerp ground-referenced heights match within a few percent on only 45 pairs. London v1 is the odd one: in Tower Hamlets Overture already has a height on 24.5 % of its buildings (against 11.3 % in the first pass's London box), and LBSM heights agree with those (median ratio 1.03); it adds a height to half of the buildings and storeys to 28 %, but only as UPRN points with no footprint (join by point), so it is a gap-filler, not a footprint source. Caveats: boxes are single neighbourhoods; point matching on large buildings and on London's mean-of-UPRN points can miss or mismatch (a LBSM TOID's height is the mean for its SCU, not one footprint); Antwerp pairs are from a 700-building sample; Rotterdam's `measuredHeight` definition is not stated on the pages read; the Rotterdam pair statistics use BAG polygons from the PDOK WFS (CC0 public-domain BAG) as footprints, matching Rotterdam attributes by BAG id, so 13 % of the ids in the box tiles (3,940 of 29,758) did not meet a BAG polygon in the box.

### 20.2 France, Switzerland, Austria

Method: Americas report 13.3. For each source, one box of 0.05 degrees (0.1 for rural Cantal); Overture release 2026-09-23.1 buildings read by the shared helper `overlap_tool.py` (row-group index `overture-index.json`, geometry as WKB); source buildings from their own services (BD TOPO WFS, Vienna WFS, Zurich WFS, Geneva ArcGIS REST, Bern GeoParquet by duckdb). "Matched" = the source building's representative point lies inside an Overture footprint (the smallest if several). "Adds a height" = the source has a height and the matched Overture building has none, or nothing matches. The right-hand column counts the other way round (Overture buildings, with the source's maximum height among the points inside each): that is the fairer view where the source is split into parts (Vienna) or roof planes. Overture's per-supplier height: the `/properties/height` source, else the footprint's dataset. Overture footprints in all boxes are OpenStreetMap (some Microsoft).

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

Reading it: every source adds a height to between two thirds (Cantal 68.6 %, Monaco 67.9 %) and 91 % (Zurich 91.1 %, Vienna 91.4 %) of the height-less Overture buildings in these boxes (Paris 87.0 %, Lyon 85.9 %, Bern 85.1 %, Geneva 77.9 %). Where both exist, Overture's mostly Microsoft heights are 1.4 to 2.2 times lower than the sources' (BD TOPO gutter heights, Swiss ridge or DSM-based heights) in Paris, Cantal, Monaco, Zurich, Geneva and Bern: a systematic offset to settle before mixing (the Americas report found the same 1.4 to 1.9 for NRCan and Toronto). Vienna is the exception (ratio 1.04; OSM heights supply 62 % of Overture's there, and the city measures eaves heights). Lyon's 20 pairs and Paris's 936 are too few to say more.
Caveats: boxes are single neighbourhoods; source heights are different quantities (see section 5); BD TOPO polygons are close to Overture's OSM footprints, so matching is high (96 %); for Vienna and Zurich the source units are parts or surfaces, which is why the per-Overture-building view is given; Monaco's box includes French communes; "bytes read" for the Overture side was not metered (the shared helper reads row groups of a few MB per box).

Method as in section 20.2: Overture release 2026-09-23.1 buildings read by duckdb from the part files whose row-group bbox statistics meet the box (the S3 listing of 512 parts and all 330,752 row-group footers were read once to build the index); a source building is "matched" when its representative point lies inside an Overture footprint (the smallest if several); GWR coordinates are LV95 points converted with PROJ (EPSG:2056 to 4326), Tyrol polygons use a point on the surface. "Adds" = the source has the value and the matched Overture building has neither height nor floors (for Tyrol: no height); only matched buildings are counted, unmatched ones would add more. Overture footprints in all these boxes are OpenStreetMap, a few Microsoft ML. Source buildings are GWR "existing" (status 1004) rows with coordinates.

Reading it: the Swiss register adds a floor count to 25-67 % of the GWR buildings that have one, in boxes where Overture has floors on 1.6-27 % of its buildings; where both have floors they are equal on 31-53 % and within one on 77-88 % (the 7.5 % equality in Zurich is the outlier). Overture's OSM heights are very uneven in Switzerland (0.5 % in Basel to 62 % in Sion). Tyrol's layer would add a height to 54-87 % of the height-less Overture buildings in its three boxes, where Overture has 0-0.5 % heights; in Innsbruck its heights run 1.69 times Overture's (106 pairs; compare the Innsbruck BEV row in 20.2, ratio 0.72 on 28 pairs).
Caveats: boxes are single neighbourhoods; GWR coordinates are points that normally lie inside the building but not always (1-14 % of the GWR points fall in no Overture footprint); GWR floors and OSM levels are different quantities; Tyrol's roof polygons are not cadastral footprints, so counts per polygon exceed counts per building (13,328 polygons against 10,512 Overture buildings in the Innsbruck box).

### 20.3 Spain, Italy

Method: Overture buildings of the box (bbox min corner inside it) read with overlap_tool.py read_box; source building's point (Catastro/Navarra/Gipuzkoa: mean of the outer ring's vertices or the label point; Bologna/Portugal: ST_PointOnSurface of the polygon) joined to the smallest Overture footprint containing it. Catastro rows count BuildingParts, so the per-building figures are the useful ones: for each Overture building, the maximum storeys of the Catastro parts inside it. "Adds storeys/height" = Overture building has neither height nor num_floors (storeys) or, for height, none at all. Boxes are 0.05 degree (Soria, San Sebastián-rural 0.1). Mean-of-vertices points can fall outside concave footprints (slightly lowers matches). Scripts and downloads were scratch and deleted.

Reading it: the Spanish cadastres are the cleanest national storeys source and match Overture's footprints at 97-98 %; they add storeys where Overture has none (Madrid 71 %, Pamplona 37 %, rural Soria 39 %). Barcelona is the exception: Overture's own floors agree with Catastro on 89 % of buildings (a link through OSM is a hypothesis, not checked), so little is added. Gipuzkoa and Bologna supply metres; as in the Americas report, their heights are 1.4-1.9 times Overture's Microsoft heights, an offset to settle before mixing (Gipuzkoa's carry status "estimated"; Bologna's are eave heights from 2001). Overture heights themselves are on 7-32 % of buildings in these boxes (Madrid 8.9 %, Gipuzkoa rural 6.9 %, Soria 16 %, San Sebastián 26 %, Pamplona 32 %). Median Overture metres per Catastro storey are 2.0-2.5, below 3 m, so Overture's heights are low or its supplier counts roofs differently.

Method: Overture buildings of the box (bbox min corner inside it) read from S3 by row-group statistics; source building point = ST_PointOnSurface, joined to the smallest Overture footprint containing it; 'adds a height' = Overture footprint with no height field holding a source building with height above 0. Source geometry reprojected from UTM 32N (EPSG:32632 or 7791) to WGS 84.

Reading it: the Lombardy DBT adds a height to 27-37 % of Overture's buildings in these boxes where Overture heights are mostly OpenStreetMap tags; DBT heights are 1.1-1.4 times Overture's (eave versus roof, 2008-2020 surveys). Milan's DBT matches only 60 % because it holds many small structures that Overture lacks. Cagliari's per-unit match is inflated by Overture's block-sized footprints.

Method as in the Americas report 13.3: one box of 0.05 degree; Overture read by duckdb (httpfs, bbox pushdown) from the one part that holds the area (part numbers found from the row-group bbox statistics of all 512 parts: Madrid part-00116, Bilbao part-00117); the source's representative point (ST_PointOnSurface, reprojected from EPSG:25830 for Madrid) tested for containment in an Overture footprint. "Matched" = point inside at least one Overture footprint. Scripts and data were scratch, deleted.

| Source and box | Source buildings | Matched to an Overture footprint | Matched with an Overture height or num_floors | Source buildings that add a height or storeys Overture lacks | Overture in the box | Bytes read |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Madrid LoD2 2D layer 01_EDIFICIO_P, -3.72..-3.67 x 40.40..40.45 | 23,845 (the height itself sits in the unread 3D file) | 22,724 (95.3 %): OSM 22,292, IGN Espana 419, Microsoft ML 13; 1,121 (4.7 %) unmatched | height on 2,233 (9.4 % of source); num_floors on 4,405 (18.5 %) | up to 21,612 (90.6 %) if every polygon has a roof height (unverified, see section 4) | 21,808 buildings: height 1,914, num_floors 4,315 | 41.4 MB (2D zip) + about 20 MB of Overture |
| Bizkaia INSPIRE buildings (storeys), Bilbao -2.95..-2.90 x 43.24..43.29 | 10,225 polygons in the box (10,283 fetched; 10,189 with floors above ground) | 9,379 (91.7 %): OSM 9,221, IGN Espana 144, Microsoft ML 14; 846 unmatched | height on 4,029 matched polygons (several cadastral polygons share one Overture footprint); num_floors on 1,053 | storeys on 9,080 (88.8 % of 10,225): source polygons with floors (10,132) not matched to an Overture footprint that has num_floors | 5,293 buildings: height 1,313, num_floors 382 | 4.4 MB (GeoJSON) + about 20 MB |

Reading it. In both boxes the source polygons are finer than Overture's (Bilbao has 10,225 cadastral polygons against 5,293 Overture buildings, so one footprint often covers several source polygons and point matching overstates matches that carry heights). Madrid's municipal base map is almost entirely already inside Overture (95 %), so its value is the roof geometry (heights), not outlines. Bizkaia's value is storeys: Overture's num_floors is on 7 % of buildings in that box.

### 20.4 Slovenia, Croatia, Cyprus

Method: 0.05-degree box, source reads from the live services (Ljubljana: 11 WFS pages of 2,000; Zagreb: 22 FeatureServer pages; Nicosia: the Zenodo GeoPackage, 285 MB, boxes transformed from CGRS93 LTM), join by representative point to the smallest containing Overture footprint (duckdb, overlap_tool.py). Overture boxes are the same parts as the other agent's sample (Ljubljana part-00206, Zagreb part-00207, Nicosia part-00219). Overture buildings in each box: Ljubljana 15,728 (192 with height, 2,807 with floors; OSM 14,577, Microsoft 1,151), Zagreb 17,911 (300; 2,314), Nicosia 27,754 (655; 2,939). "Adds a height" = source has a height and the matched Overture building has none or no footprint matches; "adds storeys" = source has storeys and Overture has neither floors nor height.

| Source and box | Source buildings | Matched to an Overture footprint | Matched with an Overture height | Source buildings that add a height Overture lacks | Storeys added | Height comparison where both | Bytes read |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Slovenia KN STAVBE, Ljubljana 14.48-14.53 x 46.03-46.08 | 21,341 (6,911 with H2 minus H1 > 0; 21,200 with storeys) | 17,704 (83.0 %); 3,637 unmatched | 290, all OSM | 6,776 = 98.0 % of its heights (31.8 % of all source buildings) | 17,444 = 82 % of source buildings | 135 pairs: median abs diff 1.1 m, signed (KN minus OSM) +0.6 m, median ratio 1.08, within 20 % 62 % | about 26 MB source (WFS GeoJSON, 6 geometry columns) plus Overture row groups |
| Zagreb ZG3D 2022, 15.95-16.00 x 45.79-45.84 | 42,869 (42,853 with Z_Delta > 0) | 27,573 (64.3 %); 15,296 unmatched | 693, all OSM | 42,164 = 98.4 % of source features | n/a (no storeys) | 689 pairs: median abs diff 4.72 m, signed -0.18 m, median ratio 0.99, within 20 % 39.9 % (OSM heights are mostly rough; ZG3D parts are not the same units as OSM buildings) | about 50 MB GeoJSON plus Overture |
| Cyprus BU3 (Zenodo), Nicosia 33.34-33.39 x 35.14-35.19 | 22,326 (22,250 with floors; no heights) | 9,111 (40.8 %); 13,215 unmatched | 311 height, 1,415 with floors | 0 (no heights in source) | 20,782 = 93.1 % | no heights to compare | 285 MB file (whole country; box read locally) plus Overture |

Reading it: Slovenia and Zagreb each supply a height for about 98 % of the heights they hold where Overture has none, but Slovenia's heights cover only a third of its buildings while ZG3D covers every feature in its box. ZG3D, 2008-vintage for two thirds of the box, will show demolished and new buildings wrongly. Zagreb's pair agreement is weak even though the median ratio is 1.0. Caveats: box = one neighbourhood each; KN's height is H2 minus H1 (a derived quantity, not a stated "height"); the ZG3D Z_Delta also includes the part's own ground-to-roof extent (parts, not buildings); the Cyprus 41 % match rate was not diagnosed (dataset projection and footprint vintage differ from Overture's OSM footprints). Ljubljana box has 21,341 KN buildings against 15,728 in Overture, so the cadastre also adds footprints.

## 22. Accidental

### 22.1 UK, Ireland, Benelux

- Overture's Amsterdam heights (31,497 buildings) are the 3DBAG heights to within 0.12 m median in the OSM record: a Dutch import of 3DBAG into OSM is the likely cause (hypothesis, import record not looked up). This means the Netherlands is largely solved for height; the mirror image of São Paulo in the Americas report.
- 3DBAG WFS answers GeoJSON with all attributes by bbox and paging (5,000 per page, about 11 MB per page): a cheap on-demand route; its numberMatched count (10,783,944) is the layer total.
- UrbIS Brussels (CC0) ships as one 488 MB GeoPackage with face-level 3D (BuildingFaces 4.5 M rows, ROOFSURFACE/WALLSURFACE/GROUNDSURFACE) readable by duckdb spatial; heights are a group-by. The monthly files go back a year in the ATOM feed.
- Luxembourg's INSPIRE GML (CC0) holds a measured heightAboveGround per building; EPSG:3034 posList order is northing-then-easting (swap before transforming); the ACT footprints GPKG (157 MB, EPSG:2169) joins to it by buildingid.
- EUBUCCO's S3 bucket is directly queryable with duckdb (no key): a quick way to get per-country shares of government-sourced heights; for NL, BE, LU the government sources are the old 3D products listed above.
- SPW Wallonia's CGU forbids redistribution to third parties and publication through a web service: both Walloon sources are unusable for a shared product but not for a personal offline copy.
- UrbIS tile-ID scheme: files are named by Lambert-72 sheet (e.g. 149170 = x 149 km, y 170 km); one GPKG covers the whole region.
- Overture carries Esri Community Maps footprints with 1,340 height entries inside the European rectangle sampled (location not found; none appeared in the 23 boxes): worth a follow-up to see which country, since it may be a municipal 3D source. Google Open Buildings also appears in Europe (7,171 sampled), likely the Cyprus / south-east area.
- OS BHA's release note counts 46,048,490 buildings, tanks, chimneys and glasshouses: the scale of a national height set (Great Britain) for comparison with 3DBAG's 10.8 M.

- London Datastore's CKAN `package_search` ignores `q` but returns all 1,310 datasets in one call (`rows=1400`), a cheap way to scan a whole catalogue by title and notes. Data Mill North behaves the same (641 datasets).
- Overture 2026-09-23.1 reads directly from `s3://overturemaps-us-west-2/release/2026-09-23.1/theme=buildings/type=building/*` with a bbox filter in about 10-16 s per box (duckdb with httpfs and spatial), so no part index is needed. duckdb now rejects the `x->` lambda form; use `lambda x:`.
- The 3DGI reconstruction is the same in two products: the Kadaster's 3D Basisvoorziening hoogtestatistieken carries `reconstructie_methode = 3dgi-lod13`, `pw_bron = ahn3` and `kwaliteits_klasse`, i.e. it is the 3DBAG pipeline; the yearly zips (1.8-2.5 GB) allow vintage comparison.
- Rotterdam 3D's 3D Tiles carry real attributes: the b3dm batch tables hold BAG id, measuredHeight, AANTAL_BOUWLAGEN (up to 102 storeys, where the 3DBAG estimate stops at 5), year of construction, building type, address; the tilesets are open without login. A box fetch is 358 small tiles.
- Antwerp publishes true 3D roof polygons (Z in metres TAW, 1.1 M parts) on a public ArcGIS server, and a ground raster that answers `identify`; the building and bridge solids of the same model are not in that service.
- Ghent's whole LoD2 model is a plain directory of 211 DWG tiles under the Flemish free-reuse licence: open, but DWG-only.
- Python's urllib failed with "Network is unreachable" (IPv6 first) on Dutch hosts where curl worked; ckan.dataplatform.nl, carto.ville.namur.be and the Gibraltar and Guernsey portals did not answer at all from this Mac.
- The PSGA Member Licence PDF is encrypted with an empty password (pypdf needs `cryptography`); its 2.1.12-2.1.13 definitions are the only personal-use route (INSPIRE End User, personal and non-commercial), which matters for City of London's INSPIRE layers.
- Tower Hamlets' LBSM v1 heights give Overture a higher height share (24.5 %) than the first pass's London box (11.3 %): Overture's height coverage varies a lot between London neighbourhoods.
- GeoDirectory's sample workbook (30 rows) is linked directly from the product page without the form; its BUILDING_HEIGHT and GROUND_HEIGHT show real lidar-like values (ground height up to 55 m).
- OS says OS OpenData (OpenMap Local) is under the OGL and prescribes its own attribution statement, "Contains OS data © Crown copyright [and database right] [year]".
- The data.overheid.nl CKAN API lives at /data/api/3/action/ (the /api/3 path is 404); it lists the Kadaster 3D records and Rotterdam 3D under creativecommons licence URLs.
- gov.im answers HTTP 200 with a 269-byte WAF "support ID" page to plain requests (not bypassed).

### 22.2 France, Switzerland, Austria

- TomTom is running OSM building-height imports (the Netherlands one is now done for Amsterdam, 136,185 buildings, 11-24 June 2026, and Overture's Amsterdam OSM heights come from it: section 19.3; France IGN BD TOPO for Paris, Netherlands 3D BAG, Japan PLATEAU, Germany Berlin Umweltatlas); France's page (synced 2026-06-23) says the Paris run found 19,541 matches, a 20-30 building pilot is next, and warns that BD TOPO's gutter height underestimates OSM height by more than 3 m on pitched roofs; if it proceeds, OSM-sourced Overture heights in Paris will rise with a gutter-height bias. The 3D BAG import carries a CC BY 4.0 waiver "to OpenStreetMap and its users" signed 2 June 2026 (suggests that CC BY 4.0 data needs a waiver to go into OSM itself). Source: https://wiki.openstreetmap.org/w/index.php?title=Organised_Editing/Activities/TomTom/Global_-_Building_Height_Imports&action=raw
- BD TOPO `batiment` already carries `identifiants_rnb`, so joining BD TOPO, RNB and BDNB needs no geometry matching; `origine_du_batiment` = "Lidar HD" marks buildings produced from the LiDAR HD cloud (30 of 1,989 in Cantal): BD TOPO will improve as that processing continues.
- BEV DLM Bauwerke for Vienna is built from the city's own FMZK (the attribute description says so), so BEV and the Vienna layer are the same measurement there.
- Bern's building-height file is GeoParquet with a bbox column: a per-tile range read is a few MB, as with NRCan in the Americas report.
- swissBUILDINGS3D 3.0's STAC items list a CityGML 2.0 zip only for EGID areas (2,184 of 6,728 items), so the national DWG/FileGDB tiles are the full-coverage option.
- Upper Austria's LoD1 shapefile carries an INSPIRE id on every polygon and 1,292,935 polygons; Styria's mask is 678,450 polygons: both are free national-scale-per-Land alternatives to the BEV file.
- Paris's "Volumes bâtis" is ODbL-native, so it can be mixed without any attribution-licence question, but has storeys only.
- The Overture Lyon box has height on 57 of 23,350 buildings: France outside Paris is nearly empty of Overture heights, so BD TOPO is the main gap-filler. Swiss Overture heights are patchy (Basel 0.5 %, Zurich 27.5 %, Lausanne 53.8 %, Sion 62 %), so a Swiss rule cannot assume either.
### 22.3 Iberia and Italy

- Barcelona's Overture floors equal Catastro's on 89 % of buildings (Madrid 45 %, Soria 95 %, Pamplona 31 %), pointing to a cadastre-derived OSM import in places.
- Gipuzkoa's heights are all marked "estimated" and run 1.6-1.9 times Overture's.
- INSPIRE Buildings across the sample: Catastro BU is 2D with storeys, Gipuzkoa BU has height and storeys, Alto Adige BU is 2D only, Catalonia's INSPIRE buildings record was seen only as a metadata entry, so INSPIRE 3D is not delivered in any of the countries' bulk downloads.
- Catastro's host omits the root certificate; the DGT product's licence field says "notspecified".
- ATOM zip for Barcelona truncated on the first download (timeout at 120 s): re-fetched at 49.7 MB.

- Milan's DBT 2020 zip holds a 1,754-file GDB and QGIS bundle (MILANO.gdb with a 'progetti vestiti' set); the layers other than UN_VOL and EDIFC were not opened.
- The Lombardy DBT schema document inside the Lecco zip (LEGGIMI_edificato.pdf, 27 pages, version 1.3 of 15 November 2013) defines UN_VOL_AV as 'altezza volume' and UN_VOL_EX as 'estrusione in quota' or 'altezza'; it is the clearest height definition found for the Italian DBT family and applies to Milan, Sondrio, Lecco and (with the same codes) the Tuscan municipal sets.
- Tuscan municipal sets also come as 'Edificio' (building) datasets: Livorno's model (id, cr_edf_is, edifc_ty, edifc_uso, edifc_sot, edifc_stat, edifc_mon, edifc_nome) has no height or storeys.
- Livorno's 28 MB zip holds every format (SHP, GML, GeoJSON, KML, RDF) plus the CC BY 4.0 legal text and an INSPIRE-style XML; the same layout is used by all ldpgis.it municipal downloads seen.
- Reaching Lombardy's open zips needed the host alias www.cartografia.servizirl.it: the www.cartografia.regione.lombardia.it URL in the catalogue answers 503 'Service Temporarily Unavailable' (Apache 2.2.15) every time. The alias is read from the same catalogue family's other records.
- Several Italian regional hosts (Piedmont, Veneto ARPAV, FVG, Trentino, Marche, Campania, Calabria, Turin) time out at TCP level from this Canadian address while others answer; whether that is geoblocking or simple outage was not tested and no route round it was tried.
- dati.gov.it's package_search is the route that worked for all catalogue reads; its 'fl' parameter breaks the answer; Overture's S3 listing gave 512 parts and the footers of all parts were read in 13 s (saved as an index in scratch).
- Sardinia's regional 2022 DBGT10K zip of all layers is 2.72 GB; its listing was read by HTTP range (remotezip) without downloading it.
- Source buildings are counted as ground-level buildings (CEDIUV, EDF_UUID, CR_EDF_UUI) with the maximum unit height for Milan, Lecco and Sondrio; Cagliari's rows are units. Overture buildings counted when the bbox min corner is in the box (0.05 degree); a source building's point is ST_PointOnSurface joined to the smallest Overture footprint containing it; heights are Overture's 'height' field, whose dataset is OpenStreetMap or Microsoft ML Buildings (first source of the record).
- Scratch downloads (about 4.7 GB under the scratch folder) were deleted at the end; the working scripts remain in the scratch folder.

- Overture's `sources` record for buildings never names the Catastro: Barcelona-box floors are all under "OpenStreetMap" (record ids like `w1092327232@3`) while 313 footprints carry "Instituto Geográfico Nacional (España)"; IGN España's records also carry num_floors in Madrid (801 of 4,315).
- Overture sources entries in the Barcelona box: 26,305 OSM, 313 IGN España, 87 Microsoft ML Buildings; 3,630 buildings have a second source entry `/properties/height` from Microsoft ML.
- Catalonia's RTT table has 8,478 buildings with `alcada` exactly 1.0 m and 914 above 100 m (max 2,578.87 m); look at both before trusting the field.
- Álava's `heightAboveGround` is exactly 3 m times the storey count in the 3,000-feature sample, so it is not a measurement; the Bizkaia service has the same INSPIRE fields but leaves heights empty.
- The Madrid LoD2 zip packaging: a 2.2 GB .zip around one .slpk compressed with Deflate64 (method 9); Python's zipfile cannot read it, and it blocks ranged reads.
- Madrid's 2D layers also include 06_ASCENSOR_P (lift shafts), 01_ELEMENTO_FUNERARIO_P (about 179,600 records by its .shx size) and 01_PORCHE_CASETA_COBERTIZO_P, i.e. sheds and porches are modelled in 3D too.
- Valencia's `geoportal.valencia.es` hosts the city's open data as ArcGIS REST (the CKAN resources are query URLs on it); Málaga's CKAN licence title says "CC BY 4.0" while its id and URL say CC BY-SA 4.0.
- GlobalBuildingAtlas (ESSD 17, 6647, 2025, TUM): LoD1 models with predicted heights for 2.68 billion buildings, footprints from OSM and Microsoft under ODbL; it covers small states such as Gibraltar and Monaco that have no open national data (the ESSD page was fetched; coverage of those states was not checked).
- Zaragoza's `idezar.zaragoza.es` resolves to node0.idezar.es (146.59.232.109) while `idezar-sig.zaragoza.es` resolves to www.zaragoza.es' address (194.224.70.75), which is the one that does not connect.

**Barcelona floors, settled**

Answer: yes, indirectly. Overture's floors there are OpenStreetMap's `building:levels`, and OSM got them from the Spanish Cadastre Buildings Import; Overture never names the Catastro.

1. Overture side (release 2026-09-23.1, part-00193, box 2.15..2.20 x 41.37..41.42, read with duckdb): 26,705 buildings, 19,997 with num_floors (75 %), 3,886 with height. Every `sources` entry is `OpenStreetMap` (26,305 footprints, ids such as `w1092327232@3`, provider osm, ODbL-1.0), `Instituto Geográfico Nacional (España)` (313 footprints) or `Microsoft ML Buildings` (87; plus 3,630 height-only entries). No dataset is named Catastro, and no `sources` entry has a per-property record for floors: the floors belong to the OSM record.
2. OSM side: 300 random OSM ways of those with num_floors (OSM API `/ways?ways=...`): 299 have `building:levels`, 46 (15 %) also `building:levels:underground`, and only 8 carry a `source` tag. The import's conversion page maps the cadastre fields exactly so: `numberOfFloorsAboveGround` (maximum over the building's parts) to `building:levels`, `numberOfFloorsBelowGround` (minimum) to `building:levels:underground` (https://wiki.openstreetmap.org/wiki/Spanish_Cadastre/Buildings_Import/Data_Conversion).
3. History side: for 60 random such ways, the changeset where `building:levels` first appears (way history, then changeset tags): 57 of 60 (95 %) have a comment `#Spanish_Cadastre_Buildings_Import ...` (for example "#Spanish_Cadastre_Buildings_Import 08900 Barcelona u00976": 08900 is Barcelona's cadastral municipality code) or the source "Dirección General del Catastro" (editor JOSM). The other 3 have other comments or sources (for example "Add building and roof levels", source Bing).
4. Import documentation: the OSM wiki proposal page (https://wiki.openstreetmap.org/wiki/Spanish_Cadastre/Buildings_Import/Proposal) says the Catastro INSPIRE licence "authorizes any use, including commercial use, provided that the cadastral information has been subject to any prior process of transformation" and that the import was conflated manually by volunteers (schedule: proposal February 2017, tool tested January 2018). The first-pass finding of 89 % agreement with the Catastro is therefore agreement with the origin, not with an independent check.
5. Contrast (same method, Madrid box, 60 ways): only 7 of 60 first-levels changesets cite the Cadastre; 26 are "Add building and roof levels" and 7 "Specify building and roof levels" (StreetComplete surveys) and so on; this fits Madrid's 45 % agreement. Overture's IGN España entries also supply 801 of Madrid's 4,315 num_floors.
6. What is not settled: whether Overture's release process applied any floors rule (the `sources` record cannot show it); the 60-way sample is small (95 % interval roughly 86-99 %); the changeset of first appearance may differ from the one that set the current value; ways only (relations were skipped); no Barcelona OSM element was compared one by one against the Catastro in this run (the 89 % figure is the first pass's).
Consequence: ODbL-mixing OSM floors that came from the Catastro is covered by the Catastro licence's transformed-product clause as the import page reads it; for a build that shipped Catastro floors directly, the first-pass row's licence reading still applies.

### 22.4 Slovenia to Cyprus

- Slovenia's WFS STAVBE also has `DELI_STAVB`, `ETAZE` (per-storey elevation and height in the register, per the study) and `ISCI_STAVBE`; a per-storey table exists but was not sampled.
- Slovenia's study (p. 44-45) counts the register: ZEN 224,023, registry entries 932,403, ZKN 22,383 buildings; only 2 % have a ZKN-style outline.
- Zagreb data portal lists 17 district datasets plus the city-wide one (ids `zg3d-*`).
- Croatian CKAN (`/ckan/api/3/action`) has a separate "Visine - INSPIRE" dataset (terrain only).
